#2277. Chelsea’s feisty friendly ends in penalty shoot-out confusion

Chelsea’s Far East tour ended in farce and confusion with players waiting around for a penalty shoot-out that never happened.

The chaos came at the end of a hard-earned, bad-tempered draw against Malaysian champions Johor Darul Ta’zim, with an own-goal in the 89th minute sparring the west London side’s blushes.

That goal also was supposed to set up penalties. But after minutes of waiting around – and Kevin Campello, Chelsea’s head of first-team operations, saying “it’s written in the contract” – the players walked off the pitch.

That confusion ended a feisty affair in which tensions threatened to boil over and that, at certain points, paid little attention to the “friendly” aspect of the clash with seven yellow cards shown. At one point the visitors’ new set-piece coach Austin MacPhee exchanged angry words with some of Johor’s coaching staff. The CFC TV commentary team suggested that the spot-kicks were abandoned because of the match’s heated atmosphere.

Austin MacPhee was ready to throw hands😂 pic.twitter.com/CFDgTOwmxE

Having played just 24 hours earlier in Jakarta – a 3-0 win over AC Milan – it was no surprise to see Xabi Alonso name a fresh XI, a mixture of players who may well be offloaded before the end of the transfer window, those needing to prove themselves, and youngsters given much-needed game time.

And considering Johor are one game away from setting a new world record of 109 matches unbeaten in the top flight – a run that would surpass the current mark set by Ivorian side Asec Mimosas in the early 1990s – it was also no shock to see the Malaysian team offer a stern, pre-season test for the west Londoners.

After a good start Chelsea were second best in a first half in which Johor should have been at least two goals to the good. The Malaysian champions controlled the first 45 minutes and were 1-0 up after 14 minutes – a low cross from the left side-footed home by Arif Aiman.

The hosts gave the young, inexperienced Chelsea backline a torrid time, especially on the flanks and Óscar Arribas was guilty of not capitalising on a great chance to double Johor’s lead, his shot after cutting in from the right going wide.

While they looked vulnerable at the back, Chelsea also struggled to keep possession in attack. But they got an equaliser they scarcely deserved after Liam Delap was manhandled to the ground in the box by Kevin Medina. The striker, who may well leave Stamford Bridge before the transfer window slams shut, fired home the spot-kick allowing the visitors to go into the break on level terms.

For the most part, the second half followed a similar pattern to the first – Jofor having the better chances, while tensions on the pitch continued to run high.

It was, however, Chelsea who took the lead after the break, Delap once again scoring from the spot, this time after Jamie Gittens was brought down in the area.

Johor, though, kept on creating, thanks in part to a fairly porous visitors’ backline, and levelled through a deflected Arribas free kick, before going 3-2 up thanks thanks to a well-taken Bérgson strike.

Had it stayed that way Chelsea could have had few complaints. But as the clock approached 90 minutes, the visitors had one last attack. Mykhailo Mudryk, just on as a substitute, delivered a cross from the left that Antonio Glauder could only steer into his own net. That was supposed to set up the penalty shoot-out that did not take place and the chance for one of the sides to claim a hollow victory.

Considering a raft of changes and that fact this was far from the first XI, it is hard to know what to make of this. But among the positive things Alonso could take from the game was the impressive form of Nicolas Jackson and good centre-forward play of Delap. The negative was, however, a poor defensive display.

Chelsea return to Stamford Bridge on Saturday for their last pre-season friendly, against Real Sociedad, before the Premier League season starts away at local rivals Fulham on August 24.

Johor Darul Ta’zim: Syihan Hazmi; Arribas, Israfilov (Carlos Akapo 82), Medina, Antonio Glauder, Silva (La’Vere Corbin-Ong 89); Arif Aiman, Petrovic, Méndez (Nené 78), Marcos Guilherme (Bérgson); Zahedi (Stoichkov 62). Substitutes: Abad, Davies, Hidalgo, Nahuel Leiva, Lowry, Mohamadou Sumareh, Stuart Wilkin.Booked: Israfilov, Antonio Glauder, Silva.Goals: Arif Aiman (14), Arribas (65), Bérgson (86).Chelsea: Sharman-Lowe (Slolina 46); Subuloye, Adarabioyo, Anselmino (Watson 29); Geovany Quenda, Dario Essugo, Nicoll-Jazuli, Gittens (Mudryk 87); Estêvão, Delap, Jacskon (Satpaev 86). Substitutes: Curd, Diakite, Kavuma-McQueen, Sarr, Walsh.Booked: Estêvão, Delap, Jackson, Geovany Quenda, Watson.Goals: Delap (42 pen, 62 pen), Antonio Glauder (89 og).

There will NOT be a penalty shootout. No one is quite sure why.

Anyway, that was an entertaining draw, plenty of good things for Chelsea (the play of Delap and Jackson) as well as worrying signs (open and vulnerable at the back).

The commentators on CFC TV are now not so sure!

That was an entertaining pre-season friendly. There was a bit of niggle and that added to the action.

There will be penalties, apparently, if it stays the same score...

Will there be a late winner? There will be four minutes of added time.

Mudryk just on picks up the ball on the left and, with four Chelsea players in the box, crosses into the danger area. It’s a good ball and asks too many questions of the hosts as Glauder diverts the ball into his own net.

It’s what the host have, on the balance of play, deserved, but it’s a gift from Chelsea. Essugo loses the ball in the final third and Bergson, on as sub pick it put driving at the box before hitting a low shot from outside the area, the ball nestling in the left corner of the net.

Chelsea have been better this half than the first 45, they’ve been able to control the pace of the game well since the break.

The pace has slowed down since the break, Chelsea seeing more of the ball. They work it right before Watson loses possession on the edge of the area.

The referee clearly forgot about the hydration break and the two teams are having a belated one now. Delightful...

I reckon there’s another goal left in this match. It’s been entertaining fare and the hosts have given as good as they’ve got, if not more.

This has been a lively match, better than most run-of-the-mill pre-season friendlies. Chelsea seeing a fair bit of possession at the moment, can they fashion a chance?

Yes they can. But it’s a longish shot from Gittens that is easily saved by Hamzi in the JDT goal.

More niggle in this match that hasn’t really had many tough challenges. After a few handbags the referee takes control and the argy bargy peters out.

It’s the least the hosts deserve. They have a free-kick 25 yards out, Arribas takes it with his right it’s at the wall but in turning away from the ball Delap deflects it into the far-right corner giving Slonina no chance.

Fluky goal but the hosts have deserved their bit of luck.

Delap again with the goal, this time he goes low, but still to the keeper’s right.

Penalty for Chelsea. Gittens cuts back in the box onto his right and is clipped by the defender. Clear-cut penalty.

Half-chance for Delap on the edge of the area. He playes a one-two with Jackson before opening up his body and letting fly, the shot going well wide.

Another good chance for the hosts and it’s not shock to write that it’s come from the industry of Arribas on the right. He fires in a low cross that evades everyone in the box.

Estevao goes down in the box, it looks as though it could well be a penalty but the referee waves play on. The Brazilian is the shown a yellow card for possibly lashing out having not got the decision he wanted.

The hosts have asserted themselves well this half, in much the same way as they did in the first 45 minutes.

Another good chance for the hosts and again it’s all too easy as Zahedi is one-on-one with the keeper. The striker, however, dithers a bit too long and Slonina makes a fine save. JDT have had plenty of chances to add to their sole goal...

Chelsea have had a ragged start to this half.

Good work from JDT who keep the ball well in midfield before a throughball is played to the on-rushing Zahedi but Slonina comes out well.

They’re back under way in Johor, and there’s an early chance for Jackson who gets the ball in the box, but the ball gets stuck underneath him and he cannot get in a good attempt in on goal.

And that is the last action of an entertaining half. Chelsea started well before the hosts opened the scoring. JDT then took control of the half and should, if truth be told, have extended their lead. But Delap did well to earn the penalty that he subsequently scored and should have made it 2-1 with the last kick of the 45.

Worrying signs at the back, however, for Alonso. Will Chelsea go to four at the back after the break?

The answer to that question (see last post) was so very nearly yes...

Jackson plays in a peach of a through ball with the outside of his right foot, Delap is one-on-one with the keeper and looks for all the world as though he has to score. BUT he scuffs his shot wide.

There will be three minutes of added time. Can Chelsea head into the break with an unexpected lead?

Free-kick for Chelsea on the JDT byline. The ball is put deep into the box, it’s headed back by Tosin before the ball is blazed over the bar.

A minute later Tosin then let’s fly form 25 yards, the ball goes well over the bar.

The hosts should have been more than 1-0 to the good and now they’re drawing. Goals change matches...

Having won the penalty Delap fires the spotkick home, sending the keeper the the wrong way.

Penalty to Chelsea and it’s all thanks to good centre-forward play by Delap. He gets the ball in the box and Medina manhands him to the ground. The referee doesn’t hesitate before pointing to the spot.

Chelsea have struggled to create anything since the opening 15 minutes. They have no control at the front and none at the back currently.

As I type that JDT have another good chance to turn the 1-0 lead into a 2-0 lead. Arribas, whose been a menace all game, dances toward the Chelsea box before delaying the pass too long.

JDT are finding a lot of joy on the flanks, there are three at the back for Chelsea and on the outside are two youngsters. The hosts are working the space well.

Yet again Chelsea are made to look decidedly average. A simple ball over the top on the right opens up the visitors’ defence and Arribas is in on goal, he cuts in and lets fly with his left peg, the shot going wide.

That should have been 2-0 to the Malaysian powerhouses.

Worrying signs for Chelsea who have struggled to keep possession up top and looked vulnerable at the back.

Yet more good work from JDT who are being given space by Chelsea. Marcos Guilherme slaloms into the west Londoners’ box and Tosin again doesn’t look the most assured but the JDT player cuts the ball back and the visitors can clear the danger.

Another good attack from the hosts who get in a shot in on goal, it’s deflected and loops into the grateful arms of Sharman-Lowe in the Chelsea goal.

There’s a break in play with Anselmino down and suffering what looks like a hamstring injury. He is eventually replaced by Reggie Watson.

Meanwhile, the home fans are in fine voice and waving several HUGE flags.

Yep, everyone’s least favourite part of the World Cup in the U, S of A is back. To be fair it’s humid in Johor so taking on fluids during the first 45 minutes is no bad idea.

There’s a bit of argy-bargy not really what you should get in a ‘friendly’. There hasn’t been a bad chellenge in the match so far so not sure where that came from...

Yet more good work from the hosts, this time it’s down the right, Arribas getting the better of Anselmino before shooting at the near post - the attempt hitting the side netting.

After a lively start Chelsea have been second best.

Poor defending from the visitors there but the chance was well snapped up by JDT.

Lovely break from the hosts. They break down the left at pace, before the ball is threaded through to the back post and Arif Aiman sidefoots into the back of the net with aplomb.

So very nearly an opener for Chelsea. They break at pace thanks to Estevao who drives down the middle before playing in Jackson. The striker cuts in from the left and shoots, the attempt is saved well by Hamzi and Estevao’s follow up is wide. Is a goal coming?

Free-kick for Chelsea some 35 yards out. Too far fro a shot on goal? Of course it is. The ball is floated into the box and the visitors earn a corner.

What can they create from this set piece? ‘Not much’ is the initial answer as the ball is cleared by JDT but Chelsea win possession back and the ball is fed to Estevao on the left, the Brazilian goes for go with his trusty left peg but the bal goes high and wide.

JDT attack down the left, it’s a lovely ball in that forces a fine header to clear the danger from Adarabioyo.

No sooner had I sent that last post than Chelsea break through the middle. The ball is fed through to Delap who gets a left-footed shot in on goal forcing a good save from the JDT keeper Hamzi.

Quiet, low-key start to the match. No real shock there, it is a pre-season friendly.

JDT give the ball away in their half and Chelsea have a half-decent platform to launch an attack from. They cannot fashion a chance and also lose possession.

They’re under way in southern Malaysia, Chelsea are in their vibrant blue and the hosts are wearing a fetching pink number. There was a late change for the visitors, Mamadou Sarr has been replaced by Olutayo Subuloye.

CHELSEA XI TO FACE JOHOR: Sharman-Lowe; Sarr, Tosin, Anselmino; Quenda, Nicoll-Jazuli, Essugo (c), Gittens; Estevao; Jackson, Delap; subs: Slonina, Curd, Subuloye, Diakite, Watson, Mudryk, Walsh, Kavuma-McQueen, Satpayev

...Luis Garcia. The Spaniard has history with Chelsea, remember the ‘ghost goal’?

Arriving for duty! 🫡 pic.twitter.com/M2XEkLC18n

Your Chelsea side today! 🔵 pic.twitter.com/0OvvGVmgpc

A mixture of players who may well be offloaded before the end of the transfer window, those needing to prove themselves, and youngsters you may not have heard of.

Johor Darul Ta’Zim – more commonly known as JDT – are the Man Utd of Malaysia (at least that’s according to a Malaysian friend of mine). In 2013, they were going nowhere and were the dictionary definition of a stagnant club, low on confidence and trophies. That was until the Crown Prince bought the club and, over the next 13 years, transformed it into the powerhouse it is today.

As if to underline JDT’s dominance over Malaysian football they are one game away from the world record unbeaten run in a top flight. The new season of the Malaysian Super League gets under way later this month and should they avoid defeat in their opening match they will extend their unbeaten run - which began in April 2021 - to 109 games and surpass the world record set by Ivorian side Asec Mimosas in the early 1990s.

Chelsea’s pre-season tour of South East Asia ends today with a match against Johor Darul Ta’zim in Malaysia. The clash comes barely 24 hours after the west Londoners beat AC Milan 3-0 in Jakarta, a much-needed win coming on the back of defeats against Juventus and Tottenham.

A fine volley from Moises Caicedo and a double from João Pedro either side of half-time led Xabi Alonso’s side to victory in the Indonesian capital.

The game was fairly even in the first half and looked set to head into the break level at 0-0 but Pedro’s header from Caicedo’s corner put Chelsea in front.

Alonso’s side then doubled their advantage seconds into the second half as Pedro scored his second, assisted by Pedro Neto.

Just minutes later, Chelsea had their third courtesy of a well-worked corner routine. Romeo Lavia’s delivery was met by Caicedo on the volley, with the Ecuadorian’s finish with the outside of the boot finding the bottom corner.

Today, the west London side face Malaysian powerhouses Johor at the Sultan Ibrahim Stadium, more on them later, but considering the tight turnaround from yesterday’s match today’s clash will provide a stern test.

“The idea, with the game against Milan and then one in Johor, was to arrive at this weekend with players not being ready for 90 minutes until now,” Alonso explained after the win in Jakarta.

“We wanted to have a logical progression in their loads; so we had our plan and we’ve stuck to that.

“We have another idea [against Johor]; that was decided yesterday [Friday]. The idea is that many young players were coming to have chances to play tomorrow. If not, we’d have been short, as playing two games in two days is quite intense. So we will need them.”

Stay here for all the team news and action with kick-off set for 1pm.

#2275. Serie A, corsa al titolo di campione d’inverno: non solo Inter e Roma, anche la Lazio (a quota 4,00) punta il traguardo che due volte su tre vale lo scudetto

Il campionato, si sa, è una lunga corsa a tappe, in cui lo scenario e la classifica possono subire stravolgimenti a ogni giornata. Ma c’è un traguardo che vale più di altri, ovvero il titolo di campione d’inverno: nella storia della Serie A, chiudere il girone d’andata in testa è valso poi lo scudetto in 64 occasioni su 94; semplificando, due volte su tre.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Non si tratta di un traguardo banale, quindi, e allora ecco che si accende la corsa: secondo i betting analyst di Goldbet e Betflag, come riporta Agipronews, la favorita per confermarsi campione d’inverno è l’Inter, proposta a quota 1,55, in calo rispetto all’1,85 di poche settimane fa.

La sfida è principalmente con la Roma, che si conferma seconda forza a 3,50, Ma la grande novità è la Lazio, che fa un enorme balzo in avanti, dal 66,00 dello scorso mese all’attuale 4,00: i biancocelesti, infatti, nel corso delle settimane si sono confermati in vetta alla classifica e hanno convinto sempre di più i bookie, che ora li vedono in piena corsa per il titolo di campione d’inverno.

Più staccate le altre squadre: si raddoppia a 8,00, infatti, per il Como, salendo ulteriormente a 12,00 per il Milan. A 25,00, infine, la Juventus.

#2274. Serie A, a Calò il premio di Player Of The Month di ottobre: alle sue spalle anche Thuram

Serie A, a Calò il premio di Player Of The Month di ottobre: alle sue spalle anche Thuram

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli FcInter1908 come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Il premio EA SPORTS FC Player Of The Month di ottobre è stato assegnato al calciatore del Frosinone Giacomo Calò. La consegna del trofeo avverrà nel pre-partita di Frosinone-Parma, in programma sabato 28 novembre alle ore 15.00 allo stadio “Benito Stirpe” di Frosinone.

I voti dei tifosi online combinati con i dati statistici individuali, hanno decretato come vincitore Giacomo Calò, che ha superato campioni del calibro di Douvikas (Como), Isaksen (Lazio), Krstovic (Atalanta), McTominay (Napoli), Thuram (Inter).

La sestina era stata selezionata tenendo conto delle analisi evolute di Kama Sport, realizzate a partire dai dati tracking registrati con il sistema Hawk-Eye. Il sistema di rating considera non soltanto i dati statistici e gli eventi tecnici, ma anche i dati posizionali. Ciò permette di analizzare aspetti cruciali come il movimento senza palla e dunque i movimenti ottimali, le scelte di gioco, il contributo all’efficienza tecnica e fisica della squadra, che permettono una valutazione oggettiva e qualitativa della prestazione. Per il calcolo finale sono state considerate le giornate dalla 6ª alla 9ª della Serie A Enilive 2026/2027.

“Giacomo Calò è una delle storie più belle e sorprendenti di questo avvio di stagione. Al suo debutto in Serie A ha saputo imporsi con personalità e qualità, dimostrando che il talento, quando incontra determinazione e lavoro quotidiano, può emergere e affermarsi a qualsiasi età – ha dichiarato l’Amministratore Delegato della Lega Calcio Serie A Luigi De Siervo –. I numeri raccontano solo una parte del suo impatto: tre gol in quattro partite a ottobre, cinque reti e un assist complessivi in campionato. Ma ciò che colpisce davvero è la capacità di interpretare il ruolo di centrocampista moderno, unendo visione di gioco, intensità e una pericolosità costante dalla distanza. L’incontro con Mister Alvini a Frosinone ha segnato la svolta di una carriera costruita con pazienza, sacrificio e fiducia nei propri mezzi. La sua parabola ci ricorda che nel calcio non esistono traguardi preclusi, ma occasioni da saper cogliere. La storia di Calò dimostra che il talento cresce dove c’è sacrificio: la Serie A premia chi non smette di crederci".

#2273. Inter, il ct dell'Albania U19 fa sognare i tifosi: "Hanno un giovane che somiglia a Musiala"

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Alen Vukaj è uno dei giovani talenti che si stanno mettendo in mostra nella Primavera dell'Inter. Il classe 2008 ha attirato l'attenzione anche in Youth League, grazie a due splendide reti realizzate contro Feyenoord e Shakhtar Donetsk. A parlare delle qualità del centrocampista nerazzurro è stato Fatjon Tafaj, commissario tecnico dell'Albania Under 19, intervistato da FCInterNews.

Il tecnico ha raccontato il suo primo approccio con Vukaj, soffermandosi sui progressi compiuti dal giovane calciatore.

LA PRIMA IMPRESSIONE SU VUKAJ – “A dire il vero, la mia prima impressione su Alen non è stata del tutto quella che mi aspettavo. In quel periodo ero stato appena nominato allenatore della squadra e, durante il primo lavoro svolto insieme in allenamento, il modo in cui si era presentato non era all’altezza delle aspettative che avevo nei suoi confronti. Bisogna considerare anche il fatto che Alen arrivasse da un infortunio, circostanza che potrebbe aver influito sulle sue condizioni fisiche e sul ritmo degli allenamenti. Nella prima partita gli ho concesso poco spazio e ha avuto a disposizione soltanto pochi minuti. Nella seconda, invece, la sua presenza in campo ha avuto un impatto decisamente positivo e ho iniziato a comprendere meglio le sue qualità e il suo potenziale. Da quel momento le sue prestazioni sono state in costante crescita e oggi posso dire che è un giocatore che si è guadagnato la mia stima e la mia fiducia”.

IL SUO PESO NELLA NAZIONALE – “Penso che Alen sia un giocatore in grado di influire positivamente sulla squadra, non soltanto grazie alle sue qualità individuali, ma anche per il modo in cui comprende e interpreta il gioco. È un calciatore che si assume le proprie responsabilità, non si nasconde nei momenti difficili e ha la capacità di prendere iniziative per aiutare la squadra”.

LE QUALITÀ TECNICHE – “Certamente, è un giocatore che ha dato un contributo importante alla Nazionale U19. Ha un livello qualitativo molto alto e lo considererei uno dei giocatori tecnicamente più forti della nostra squadra. Sa risolvere molto bene le situazioni di uno contro uno e di uno contro due ed è molto intelligente”.

#2272. Bove, il McDonald's portato da Cataldi e il legame con la Fiorentina

Nell’intervista per la presentazione del docufilm, ricorda anche il risveglio dopo il malore e il sostegno di Cobolli.

Danilo Cataldi andava a trovare Edoardo Bove in ospedale e gli portava di nascosto del McDonald’s durante il ricovero seguito al malore del 1° dicembre 2024, avvenuto in Fiorentina-Inter. Bove lo ha raccontato in un’intervista a Sky Sport 24 per la presentazione di In «Un Battito», il docufilm dedicato a quanto gli è accaduto e al percorso successivo al trauma. Ha parlato anche della Fiorentina, della convivenza con il defibrillatore e del sostegno dell’amico tennista Flavio Cobolli.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

L’ex Roma ha ripercorso ciò che ricorda di quel giorno, a partire dal risveglio: «Avevo vent'anni quando mi sono trovato ad affrontare qualcosa che ha stravolto la mia vita. Mi sono ritrovato in una situazione completamente nuova e ho dovuto imparare a capire come proseguire. In un certo senso, potermi fermare e ragionare su quello che stava succedendo è stata una fortuna. Del 1° dicembre 2024 ricordo soprattutto il momento del risveglio e la gioia nel vedere le reazioni dei miei familiari».

Bove ha spiegato di non avere ricordi del malore e di convivere tranquillamente con il defibrillatore. Ha affrontato anche le paure legate al ritorno in campo, scegliendo il supporto di una mental coach e di uno psicoterapeuta: «Non ricordo quello che è successo durante il malore, perché non ho vissuto direttamente quei momenti. Convivo tranquillamente con il defibrillatore e mi auguro che non mi riservi altre sorprese. Non ha modificato la mia personalità né mi ha impedito di affrontare il percorso. Sapevo che, una volta tornato a giocare, avrei potuto avere delle paure: per questo ho deciso di affidarmi a una mental coach e a uno psicoterapeuta, per riuscire a superare il trauma. Adesso sono più tranquillo e vivo il calcio con una consapevolezza nuova. Ho compreso che non può rappresentare l'unico centro della mia vita: esistono tante altre cose importanti che, forse, prima consideravo scontate».

La Fiorentina è rimasta vicina a Bove nei mesi successivi al malore. Il calciatore ha ricordato il rapporto con la squadra e la possibilità di continuare a seguirla anche senza scendere in campo: «Per me è stata una seconda famiglia. Mi è rimasta vicino nel periodo più difficile che abbia mai attraversato e sarò sempre grato a tutte le persone che mi hanno dato una mano. Mi hanno consentito di continuare a seguire la squadra anche quando non avevo la possibilità di scendere in campo. I compagni non mi hanno mai fatto sentire messo da parte e, pur essendo lontano dal terreno di gioco, hanno continuato a farmi sentire parte del gruppo. Sono riconoscente alla società e a tutto lo staff: è un rapporto che conserverò per sempre».

#2267. Musmarra: "A gennaio serve uno alla Acerbi. Faccio questo nome italiano, davvero sottovalutato"

Musmarra: "A gennaio serve uno alla Acerbi. Faccio questo nome italiano, davvero sottovalutato"

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

John Stones fin qui non ha dato le garanzie (soprattutto fisiche) che cercava l'Inter e dunque, in questi giorni, si stanno moltiplicando le voci su un possibile intervento in difesa dei nerazzurri nel prossimo mercato di gennaio. Sul suo canale YouTube, Alfio Musmarra si è espresso così sull'argomento:

"Continuo ad avere dei dubbi su Stones. L'Inter sapeva che era imperfetto fisicamente, ma voleva un calciatore che alzasse il livello nei big match. Speriamo che da qui in avanti Stones possa fare quel percorso. Altrimenti, l'Inter dovrebbe forse andare a prendere un difensore dalle caratteristiche che adesso mancano, ovvero quel marcatore cattivo che si attacca alla maglia degli avversari e non lo lascia respirare e che, al contempo, alza i compagni. Quello che faceva un po' Francesco Acerbi. E' quello che manca a questa squadra. Non so se sia facile da trovare, i dirigenti dovranno essere bravi a setacciare il mercato. Non so se si possa prendere a gennaio, dipende anche dalle condizioni di Stones".

"L'Inter dovrebbe fare una pescata alla Balerdi, a meno che non si tenti di fare una genialata. Io un nome ce l'avrei: tante volte si cercano nomi esotici, all'inizio per esempio nessuno pensava Acerbi potesse fare quelle cose. Ora, non dico che sia a quel livello lì, ma Baschirotto è italiano, è uno che dietro ha sempre fatto bene, è in una situazione particolare: ma da gennaio a giugno non potrebbe dare una mano eventualmente? E' un nome che mi è venuto in mente perché è forte fisicamente, esuberante, con qualità. Tante volte ci fossilizziamo sui nomi enormi, ma a volte li hai in casa e ti risolve tante situazioni. Lui potrebbe essere una soluzione intelligente, è uno dei più sottovalutati del nostro campionato".

#2266. Andoni Iraola: Liverpool cannot ‘sustain the level’ I want

Andoni Iraola has conceded that Liverpool cannot currently “sustain the level” he expects after his side squandered a two-goal lead for the second match in a row.

In the Spaniard’s first game at Anfield since becoming manager, Monaco fought back to win 3-2 by virtue of Paris Brunner’s late glancing header seven days on from Leeds United’s 4-2 victory in Chicago, emphasising Liverpool’s alarming lack of depth, particularly in defence.

“We played a good first half, especially a good 30 minutes. Right now we probably don’t have much more, in terms of the levels required to play like this. They deserved to turn around the game.

“We won the first two pre-season games, but the last two we cannot sustain the level we want for all of the game. As soon as we made some changes, players start to get tired and we cannot sustain that level. So, we have work to do.”

Alex on target 🎯 pic.twitter.com/PT6AYrLxyz

Alisson, Virgil van Dijk and Cody Gakpo all made their first appearances and contributed to a lively first-half in which Iraola’s commitment to man-to-man marking and an intensive pressing structure were evident out of possession. With the ball in the first half they demonstrated the kind of slick passing that Bournemouth employed to carve opponents open on the counter-attack.

A nimble one-two between Ryan Gravenberch and Dominik Szoboszlai allowed the Dutch midfielder to set up Alexander Isak to score with a shot from 16 yards and the most expensive player in English football history laid claim to the second, too, when he seemed to hook in a rebound from Lukas Hradecky’s save while lying in the six-yard box.

Later he would graciously acknowledge that he and Florian Wirtz had connected with the ball simultaneously and he was happy to yield the credit to his team-mate.

Our second goal of the game, scored by Wirtz… we think 🤔 pic.twitter.com/FUzA363Oxw

At that point it seemed that victory would be a cakewalk but just before half-time Aleksandr Golovin caught Van Dijk out with a feint and late swerve to the right, tripping over the Liverpool captain’s clumsy hack. Despite a heavy limp, Golovin hammered the penalty past Alisson’s dive to make it 2-1 at the break.

Iraola rationed his early changes at the start of the second half to two but Liverpool could not regain their rhythm and Mika Biereth capitalised on a Gravenberch error to score the equaliser after battling to be first to the rebound when Giorgi Mamardashvili saved his first effort, stroking his effort through Wataru Endo’s legs.

Cue five substitutions as soon as that goal had gone in and a raft more followed, killing the remaining half-hour of all momentum and cohesion. By the time Brunner exploited a dozy mix-up between Milos Kerkez and Federico Chiesa about whose responsibility he was at a corner to flash his header past Mamardashvili, only two of the 22 starters were still on the pitch.

Liverpool: Alisson (Mamardashvili 46), Tsimikas (Kerkez 59), Van Dijk (c) (Chambers 59), Ndukwe (Endo 46), Frimpong (Ramsay 75), Gravenberch (Nyoni 59), Gakpo (Ngumoha 59), Wirtz (McConnell 59), Koumas (Chiesa 75), Isak (Muñoz 59)Substitutes: Morrison, Davies.Goals: Isak (16), Wirtz (29)Monaco: Hradecky, Nazinho (Mawissa 75), Dier (c) (Kehrer 75), S Sané, Vanderson (Teze 46), Golovin (Idumbo 75), Cabral (Soubeir 75), Camara (Benzahra 64), Detourbet (Coulibaly 64), Biereth (Brunner 80), Abline (Konaté 64).Substitutes: Assab, Kohn, Lienard, Nibombe, Touré.  Goals: Golovin (44 pen), Biereth (56), Brunner (88)

They played well in the first half, as they did against Leeds, but lost all shape and structure in the second half to lose 3-2.

Yellow card for Konate for clattering into Endo late. That’s the last of the action.

Liverpool 2 Monaco 3 (Brunner)  Brilliant near-post glancing header from a devilishly whippy, dipping corner. Kerkez doing his best Rip van Winkle impression.

Diligent defensive work by Ngumoha stops Konate’s run down the outside left.

A final substitution, hopefully. Brunner replaces Biereth. Konate’s shot, a pearoller, is easily saved by Mamardashvili after A mix-up between Kerkez and Munoz.

As usual, the substitutions have killed the game. Watched Leeds vs RB Leipzig yesterday. They played two 60 minute matches which kept it more competitive for longer but still descended into a kickabout late on.

Mawissa, Kehrer, Idumbo and Soubeir have replaced Nazinho, Dier, Golovin and Cabral.

Monaco also made changes, four I think. Will catch up with then once they’re confirmed. Ramsey is into the game straight away tp stop Coulibaly’s shot.

Ramsay, Chiesa and Elliott are coming on for Szoboszlai, Frimpong and Koumas. Lovely applause for Elliott.

Konate sweeps a left-foot shot from the left of the box past the right post with Mamardashvili forced into a headlong dive. Liverpool have lost their defensive shape and intensity, allowing Monaco to punch holes through the lines with some direct running.

Nazinho gets round Frimpong down the Monaco left and cuts back the perfect pass to put it on a plate for Coulibaly who hammers a left-foot shot from the right of the D that Mamardashvili throws up his hands to block. That would have stung. Good save. A flag would have meant it wouldn’t have counted but even so Frimpong’s defensive work wasn’t good enough.

It’s been very scrappy since the changes but then Ngumoha pounces on to a loose ball, glides up the right and tries to play Koumas in behind the back four. Sadly, he overhits the pass which trickles out for a goal-kick. Cue an ovation and cries of ‘Rio! Rio!’

Three changes for Monaco with Detoubet, Abline and Camara departing for Benzhara, Coulibaly and Konaté.

Ngumoha has gone to the right wing and Koumas is playing through the middle as a false nine.

Liverpool make five changes with Gakpo, Van Dijk, Wirtz, Tsimikas and Isak departing while Munoz makes his ‘debut’ with Ngumoha, Nyoni, Chambers, McConell and Kerkez.

Liverpool 2 Monaco 2 (Biereth) Mistake by Gravenberch allows Golovin to play a pass into the left half of the box for Biereth. His first shot is well saved by Mamardashvili at full stretch but his tenacity allowed him to get to the rebound ahead of Endo, swivel and stick his second effort in to equalise.

Monaco overplay at the edge of the box, Detourbet, Biereth and Abline playing ‘after you. Claud’, instead of shooting.

A slick, short passing move between Gravenberch, Wirtz and Isak ends with Isak on the deck in the box after Gravenberch’s step-over allowed the ball to run to him. Isak took a touch to move the ball on to his left but gave the defender a glimmer of it and he hacked it away before felling Isak as well as collateral damage.

Quiet start to the half. Biereth just pressed Mamardashvili but the keeper managed to sweep the ball away even though under more pressure than he would have expected.

Liverpool try to play out from the back with Mamardashvili spearing a straight pass to Gravenberch who tried to knock it round the corner for Endo but the ball skidded under his studs and out for a throw-in.

Isak is apparently claiming both goals. Replays are indistinct. He could have clipped it in off his heel as he had a swing at the ball while grounded at the same time as Wirtz.

Our second goal of the game, scored by Wirtz… we think 🤔 pic.twitter.com/FUzA363Oxw

Endo has slotted in at centre-back alongside Van Dijk.

Half-time changes: Mamardashvili for Alisson and Endo for Ndukwe.

For Monaco, Teze for Vanderson at right-back.

Much to admire in that first-half performance from Liverpool’s front six and centre-backs, the latter two particularly out of possession and winning it back. The man-to-man system is working well for them. But they should be out of sight by now instead of leading by only a goal.

Liverpool free-kick on the right after a foul on Koumas. Szoboszlai whips in the cross and Van Dijk gets up first to the left of the penalty spot but bludgeons his header about a foot wide of the right post.

Charlie Adam says the penalty was ‘a soft one’ but Van Dijk did not adjust when Golovin shifted it to his right, stuck out a leg and Golovim understandably decided to topple over the hurdle.

Liverpool 2 Monaco 1 (Golovin, pen) Speared it into the top right. Alisson went the right way but was beaten for pace and power.

The referee, Jarred Gillett, gives Monaco a penalty. It was deserved, too. Golovin went on a driving run down the middle, shifted it to his right and was tripped by Van Dijk.

Liverpool are well worth their lead. They are streets ahead of Monaco.

A microcosm of the game so far: Liverpool pass goes astray, a player wins it back – this time Gakpo – by snapping into a tackle before laying it off to his left for the unmarked Tsimikas to skim a low shot about a foot wide of the left post from 25 yards.

Monaco have done very little since that wee salvo in the opening minute but just now Abline was given too much space from distance but whacked his shot into the Kop.

Big cheer from the crowd for Hugo Ekitike

The impressive Koumas diddles Nazinho with a Cruyff turn but then thumps his shot straight into Hradecky’s chest.

Liverpool 2 Monaco 0 (Wirtz) Pass chipped out to Koumas down the right who arcs a cross to the back post. Gakpo meets it with a header back across goal to Isak whose first effort from a couple of yards is blocked but the ball gets stuck under Isak’s falling body and Wirtz is Johnny on the Spot to stab it in from a 18 inches as Dier tried to scramble it away.

Liverpool have been impressive so far but if there has been a weakness it’s still the balance in central midfield defensively. Going forward both Gravenberch and Szobiszlai have dovetailed together well. They win the ball back when Van Dijk steps out and the midfield two fly forward to tee up Koumas for a shot that strikes Dier.

Chance for Wirtz, who opens his body from the left of the D to try to bend a shot into the bottom right corner but fizzes it the wrong side of the post.

It’s been pretty breathless since the goal, Liverpool dozily letting Monaco through the middle before twice cutting Monaco apart in midfield only for Isak to overrun a pass in the box delivered by Wirtz and then Koumas to shoot over.

Liverpool 1 Monaco 0 (Isak) Chip to Gakpo down the left of the box, he clips it first time back to Gravenberch in the D who lays it off to Szoboszlai. Ping-ping-ping. Szoboszlai keeps the tempo high with return, the one in the one-two with Gravenberch who picks out Isak to bury a shot from about 15 yards into the bottom left corner.

Alex on target 🎯 pic.twitter.com/PT6AYrLxyz

Excellent move from Liverpool, instigated by Ndukwe’s fine tackle on Biereth. He knocks it short to Gravenberch who arrows a 60-yard pass out to Koumas hurtling down the right. He measures his cross perfectly for Gakpo stealing in off the left wing and though he connects powerfully with the meat of his brow, he bashes it straight at the keeper. He was offside in any case, it transpires.

Charlie Adam on co-comms points out that Liverpool are going man-to-man now in classic Iraola/Bielsa style and when the camera goes high you can see it, especially with the full-backs.

Liverpool are carving the middle of the park up with incisive passing. Szoboszlai starts a move that sends Isak down the left of the box. He traps the chip, turns back to face away from goal and taps a pass back to Wirtz who gallops on to it and bends a shot that Hradecky dives low to his left to beat away with a firm left wrist. Frimpong pounces on the rebound and clips a tame effort straight at Hradecky who had quickly restored himself to the vertical.

Van Dijk gobbles up a long pass over Ndukwe, snuffing out any possibility of Biereth getting there first.

Tsimikas is free down the left but overhits his cross on the run when Isak was begging for a cut-back. The left-back went for the back post and ballooned his centre behind, miles beyond Koumas and Wirtz.

Liverpool defend the corner solidly and Frimpong races away on the counter, bombing on to the ball and sprinting into the Monaco half. But his touch is not as sound as his pace and Monaco deal with him when he fluffs his control.

A period of applause in memory of Kevin Keegan ❤️ pic.twitter.com/AkPZG7babe

Liverpool kick off defending the Kop. They roll the ball back to Frimpong who chips a long pass up the right to Koumas who hares on to the pass down the right and crosses, just beyond Isak. Hradecky gathers and bowls the ball out. A mistake by Tisimikas lets Biereth barrel through the inside-right to hammer a shot that Alisson turns behind for a corner.

The crowd applauds in the modern way. My goodness he was a terrific player. Saw him play for Liverpool, Southampton and Newcastle. Absolutely relentless.

Virgil van Dijk has the pennant. Liverpool are wearing all red with the paint splashes of the 1990 kit. Monaco are in a very pale green.

For me it’s a special game, most of the players and people will be ‘it’s [just] a pre-season friendly’, but for me it’s a lot more than this. It will be the first time I have the chance to go out, to feel this stadium and I hope we can also perform well.

It’s a good game for us, because it’s going to be demanding, it’s a team that wants to dominate and they also want to press high so we will see how we are.

I think the cases of Ali[sson], Cody, and Virgil I think it’s their first game. It shouldn’t go longer than 45 minutes, maybe a little bit more if they feel comfortable.

Also for some of the others Ryan, Flo, and Alex, it’s the second game so we have to be aware of those things.

But the league starts in two weeks, so we cannot wait also too much; we have to give them quality minutes.

I think today is going to be a good reference, two games – Monaco and Como – teams that try dominate the game, they are very good in possession, so they will demand us the best without the ball. If we want to control the game and also physical teams, they are probably a very good test.

I hope we are at the level and we can do very good games.

Liverpool Alisson; Frimpong, Ndukwe, Van Dijk, Tsimikas; Szoboszlai, Gakpo; Koumaz, Wirtz, Gakpo; Isak.

Monaco Hradecky; Vanderson, Dier, Sané, Nazinho; Detourbet, Cabral, Camara, Golovin, Abline, Biereth.

Le 𝐗𝐈 qui débutera cette rencontre face à 𝐋𝐢𝐯𝐞𝐫𝐩𝐨𝐨𝐥 📋 pic.twitter.com/2cOYRbzUEN

🥳 Feliz aniversário, 𝐅𝐢𝐥𝐢𝐩𝐞 𝐋𝐮𝐢́𝐬! 🎉Notre coach fête aujourd'hui ses 41 ans 🎂 pic.twitter.com/VTd4BQ5rRK

Alisson, Van Dijk and Gakpo return, Ndukwe continues in defence.

The Reds to take on AS Monaco at Anfield!Watch the match LIVE on All Red Video 📺

Good afternoon and welcome to live coverage of the first of two Liverpool home friendlies after their preseason tour of the United States. Andoni Iraola’s side saw off Wrexham and Sunderland in their first two matches but gave away a two-goal lead at half-time in the final fixture and ended up being beaten 4-2 by Leeds United in Chicago in the last game after multiple changes. What did we learn? Not a huge amount in the absence of Virgil van Dijk, Alisson, Víctor Muñoz, Cody Gakpo and Alexis Mac Allister, the first four of whom are available today other than an ongoing vulnerability from set-pieces and Curtis Jones looking like he is in desperate need of a fresh start.

This afternoon at Anfield they take on AS Monaco who finished seventh in Ligue 1 this season but are under new management with the recruitment of the former Chelsea and Atlético left-back Filipe Luís as head coach. It’s quite the coup for Le Rocher given he led Flamengo to their first Brazilian title in five years, the Copa Libertadores and the Copa do Brasil in his 18 months in charge.

In pre-season so far he has overseen a 5-2 win over fourth-tier AS Saint-Priest, a 2-0 defeat by Sporting Clube, a 2-2 draw with Cercle Bruges and a 1-0 victory over Getafe. They have sold their best player, Maghnes Akliouche, to PSG for £43m and Aladji Bamba to Newcastle for £34m but have so far retained Mamadou Coulibaly and Folarin Balogun, signed and made Ansu Fati’s loan move into a long-term deal.

Iraola may well persevere with Trey Nyoni in midfield who, after his performances on tour, ought to be on the threshold of a breakthrough season and it would be interesting to see him paired with Ryan Gravenberch just behind Dominik Szoboszlai.

Ifeanyi Ndukwe, who played well in America after coming on for the injured Joe Gomez, does not have a UK work permit and will have to go abroad on loan for the season but Jérémy Jacquet, who has yet to feature, was due to have a fitness test this morning and would be a welcome to boost to a squad whose defence still requires significant reinforcements.

#2263. Lazio, Belahyane e il Marocco: «Spero di poter giocare qualche minuto»

Richiamato dopo due anni, il centrocampista pensa di poter aiutare la squadra con grinta e fisico e ringrazia i tifosi.

Reda Belahyane è stato richiamato dal Marocco a due anni dall’ultima convocazione. Il centrocampista della Lazio ha raccontato la gioia per il ritorno in nazionale ai microfoni della Federazione, tramite il canale ufficiale di youtube.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Belahyane era impegnato ad allenarsi quando è uscita la lista. Ha saputo della chiamata dai familiari e ha ricordato le sue due precedenti convocazioni: «La convocazione? Ho visto la lista più tardi di tutti perché mi stavo allenando. È stata la mia famiglia a dirmelo. Ero molto felice, soprattutto perché avevo già ricevuto solo due convocazioni in Nazionale prima di questa. Essere richiamato è stato bellissimo».

Il centrocampista vorrebbe trovare spazio e ritiene di poter contribuire con grinta e fisico: «Spero di poter giocare qualche minuto: credo di poter dare una mano al gruppo ed essere un valore aggiunto per la squadra, soprattutto in termini di grinta e fisico».

#2261. Il Bruges spara alto per Tresoldi, Martoretti: "Se il Milan è disposto a spendere..."

Nel corso del consueto appuntamento con Maracanà, programma in onda sulle frequenze di TMW Radio, il procuratore Giocondo Martoretti ha fatto un po' il punto sui nomi più interessanti in vista del prossimo calciomercato invernale, soffermandosi anche su quello di Nicolò Tresoldi, finito nel mirino del Milan e di mezza Europa. Di seguito il suo pensiero.

Cardinale pare che voglia investire ancora per il Milan nella sessione del mercato invernale. Tresoldi dal Brugge potrebbe essere un colpo all'altezza? "L'impatto che ha avuto Malen a Roma lo scorso Gennaio fa capire quanto un attaccante bravo possa elevare tutto l'insieme di una squadra. La bravura della società e degli operatori sta nello scouting, è lì che non bisogna sbagliare. Certo, poi a Gennaio giocatori importanti si spostano difficilmente. Tante volte però si può sempre migliorare con le giuste operazioni. Tresoldi comunque si è presentato alla grande ai tifosi italiani, segnando tre gol all'Inter. Ha una potenzialità enorme e se la società è disposta a spendere molti milioni questa operazione può andare in porto. Lui e Endrick, che era stato accostato precedentemente, hanno caratteristiche molto diverse. Ora devi capire su che tipo di profilo puntare".
